396490Lehmbruck, Wilhelm [Artist / Creator]Hagen Torso
1910/1911 -o.D. Lehmbruck; Hugo Simon Collection (1880-1950); 1933, 12.6. returned by the Nationalgalerie ‘Lehmbruck, Torso’ to the owner Simon; 2.4.1938 public art collect. Basel (list Zurich 1934); 1941 Lucerne (estimate Fr. 5,000.-); 1950, 01.11. ‘Furthermore, to my knowledge, the following paintings are still in the collection.’; 1950, 27.12. Kunstmuseum Basel, Depot; 1951 -August sold to Werner Bär, Zurich; until 2004 in the Werner Bär Collection; 2004, 3/4.2. Sotheby's, London; whereabouts unknown

584347Samuel ben Zwi Hirsch Dresnitz [Author]Kitzur Maʿavar Yabok
In 1748, commissioned by the Chevrah Kaddishah (Burial Society) of the Jewish Community in Nikolsburg (NJC), in active use until 1929; In 1929, selected for the NJC “treasury,” a special collection of rare objects no longer in use for ritual purposes; In 1935, loaned by the NJC to the newly established Central Jewish Museum for Moravia and Silesia in Nikolsburg (CJMMS); In May 1938, shortly after the Anschluss of Austria, evacuation of the CJMMS collection inland, temporary storage at the Jewish Community in Brno (BJC); Late April/Early May 1942 transfer of the collection from BJC to the Jewish Religious Community in Prague (PJC), however, without the Kitzur ma’avar Yabok manuscript (Manuscript) which goes missing before March 31, 1942; the Manuscript is allegedly held by Rabbi Albert Schön and/or another person for unknown motives and under unknown circumstances; On March 31, 1942 Rabbi Schön’s deportation to the Theresienstadt ghetto and later, in 1944, to Auschwitz-Birkenau where he is murdered; In or shortly after 1945 transfer of the Manuscript to the possession of Ruth Joklova, Albert Schön’s sister-in-law, from an unknown source and under unknown circumstances; Between 1968-1972, illicit exportation of the Manuscript from what was then Czechoslovakia and illicit import to the U.S.; In September 2010, put for an auction with Sotheby’s New York; In October 2010, withdrawn from sale upon request of the Jewish Museum in Prague (JMP), however, returned to the consignor, Ruth Joklova’s descendant; January 2016, long-term loan to the Los Angeles Museum of the Holocaust (LAMOTH); November 2017 return to the lender; current location unknown.
https://www.lostart.de/en/Verlust/584347 ↗

172705Unknown (Goebele zugeshrieben) [Artist / Creator]Quail and gun dog
History of loss: Ehrenbreitstein Fortress, March 1945-03 to October. Allegedly destroyed during the war. Officially, the accusation that French and American army personnel could have stolen the objects from the depot at Ehrenbreitstein Fortress was never investigated. The suspicion that at least some of the objects were taken to France and the USA is confirmed by two events: The Madonna from the ‘Carinthian Annunciation’ (inventory P 937) was acquired in France by O. Wertheimer in 1948. He returned the sculpture to the Middle Rhine Museum. And the Lucas van Leyden painting ‘Feeding the multitude’, winged altarpiece (inventory M 1968/67) appeared at a Sotheby's auction in 1968. The consignor was represented by American lawyers. The museum eventually regained possession of the painting; Responsible: French/American (?/?); Organisation: French/US. (?/?) ; Organisation: Military administration. Auslagerung (Ehrenbreitstein, Festung)
https://www.lostart.de/en/Verlust/172705 ↗

Wertheimer in 1948. He returned the sculpture to the Middle Rhine Museum. And the Lucas van Leyden painting ‘Feeding the multitude’, winged altarpiece (inventory M 1968/67) appeared at a Sotheby's auction in 1968. The consignor was represented by American lawyers. The museum eventually regained possession of the painting; Responsible: French/American (?/?); Organisation: French/US. (?/?) ; Organisation: Military administration. Auslagerung (Ehrenbreitstein, Festung)
https://www.lostart.de/en/Verlust/171379 ↗

And the Lucas van Leyden painting ‘Feeding the multitude’, winged altarpiece (inventory M 1968/67) appeared at a Sotheby's auction in 1968. The consignor was represented by American lawyers. The museum eventually regained possession of the painting; Responsible: French/American (?/?); Organisation: French/US. (?/?) ; Organisation: Military administration. Auslagerung (Ehrenbreitstein, Festung)

The museum eventually regained possession of the painting; Responsible: French/American (?/?); Organisation: French/US. (?/?) ; Organisation: Military administration. Auslagerung (Ehrenbreitstein, Festung)

172807Unknown [Artist / Creator]The holy trinity
History of loss: Ehrenbreitstein Fortress, March 1945-03 to October. Allegedly destroyed during the war. Officially, the accusation that French and American army personnel could have stolen the objects from the depot at Ehrenbreitstein Fortress was never investigated. The suspicion that at least some of the objects were taken to France and the USA is confirmed by two events: The Madonna from the ‘Carinthian Annunciation’ (inventory P 937) was acquired in France by O. Wertheimer in 1948. He returned the sculpture to the Middle Rhine Museum. And the Lucas van Leyden painting ‘Feeding the multitude’, winged altarpiece (inventory M 1968/67) appeared at a Sotheby's auction in 1968. The consignor was represented by American lawyers. 629499Matisse, Henri [Artist / Creator]Intérieur
Bernheim Jeune; Hotel des Ventes (Paris); Alfred Lindon collection, acquired from above; looted on 5 Dec, 1940, from Chase Safe Deposit Company, 41, rue Cambon, Paris; transferred on 10 Dec, 1940, to Jeu de Paume; sent by Rochlitz to Goering from Paris to Baden-Baden in June 1944 and from there it was lost. On May 5 , 1941 the art dealer Gustav Rochlitz exchanged three ‘degenerate’ paintings by Matisse and Renoir for a painting of the School of Fontainebleau. The Matisse and one of the Renoirs belonged to Alfred Lindenbaum. There is no record as to whom the other Renoir belonged. Possibly bought by Feilchenfeldt on the French market around 1984; sold at Sotheby's London, 5 Feb 2002, lot 17; current whereabouts unknown

570368Unknown [Artist / Creator]Lidded cup with "belly band"
Überweisung 1965. Freistaat Bayern. Gemäß der Vereinbarung vom 06.12.1960 zwischen der Bundesrepublik Deutschland und dem Freistaat Bayern. Von der Treuhandverwaltung für Kulturgut übernommen vom Central Collecting Point München aus der Vermögenseinziehung Hermann Göring. Provenienzforschung: In den Datenbanken zum Central Collecting Point und der Treuhandverwaltung für Kulturgut keine Angaben zur Vorprovenienz. Laut Restitutionskartei war Walter Andreas Hofer die Provenienz unbekannt. 1883-1886 im Besitz von Karl Freiherr von Rothschild, Frankfurt. Nach dessen Tod und der Aufteilung des auf der Günthersburg in Frankfurt befindlichen Kunstbesitzes an die Töchter am 09.12.1886 als Nachlass-Nr. 170a an Freifräulein Hannah Louise von Rothschild, Frankfurt, vererbt. Am 14.01.1895 mit der Aufteilung des Erbes von Freifräulein Hannah Louise von Rothschild als Nachlass-Nr. 11 an deren Schwester Emma, Lady Rothschild, London, vererbt. Nicht in der Auktion des Nachlasses von Lady Rothschild durch ihren Sohn Victor Rothschild bei Sotheby's London vom 26.-28.04.1937. Der Verbleib des Deckelbechers nach der Aufteilung 1895 ist bislang unbekannt. Zu unbekanntem Zeitpunkt aus unbekanntem Eigentum/Besitz in die 'Sammlung Göring'. Aufgrund der Provenienzlücke(n) kann ein NS-verfolgungsbedingter Entzug nicht ausgeschlossen werden.
https://www.lostart.de/en/Fund/570368 ↗

209706Sprüngli, Hans Jakob [Artist / Creator]Bowl (Tazza)
According to the 1721 inventory, the object was a gift by Charles William, margrave of Baden-Durlach to duke Frederick II of Saxe-Gotha-Altenburg. 1945 withdrawn from the museum. The tazza was discovered by the London art dealer Rainer Zietz at an auction of Sotheby's Amsterdam ( November 4, 2002) and identified as former Gotha collection property. The consignor agreed to return the piece to Gotha Castle. The handover by Sotheby's took place on 06.12.2002 in Gotha. Status restituted

1277Wtewael, Joachim Antonisz [Artist / Creator]The Holy Family with St John, St Elisabeth and the Angels
Bought under duke August of Saxe-Gotha-Altenburg (cf. 1826 paintings inventory). The painting was stolen by a member of the Red Army at the beginning of 1946 (from Reinhardsbrunn or the Ducal Museum); via several intermediate stations auction house Sotheby's London in 1992. The right of ownership was decided in a trial lasting several years, which can be regarded as a precedent, since Hon Justice Moses decided at the end of 1998 that the claim for restitution to the original owner was not time-barred and that the work should therefore be returned to Gotha. On loan from the Federal Republic of Germany since 1999. Status restituted
https://www.lostart.de/en/Verlust/1277 ↗
109444Dou, Gerrit [Artist / Creator]The Spinner
According to Hofstede de Groot, the painting was sold to Barend Kooi on June 11, 1800 at Jan Gildemeester Jansz. in Amsterdam (lot. no. 38, for 1220 guilders) and then, on July 31, 1811 in Dordrecht to B. de Haart by A. v. d. Werff from Zuidland (lot. no. 25, for 1300 guilders); Rathgeber passes on that duke August of Saxe-Gotha-Altenburg acquired the painting from the art dealer Palmer; bought in 1819, for the sum of 13,000/15,000 thalers. Exhibited in room 12 in 1935 (reorganization 1935); transferred to Reinhardsbrunn in 1943 (catalog 1943, sheet 168); brought to Coburg by Eberhard Schenk zu Schweinsberg by messenger in 1945 (ThHStA Weimar, file C 1624, fol. 139: transcript of 28.4.1953); sold at Sotheby's in 1987; auctioned by Christie's in 1996 from private collection; current location: collection Dr. August Oetker, Bielefeld. Auslagerung (Reinhardsbrunn, Jagdschloss)
